# Preface

The prominent advance of human civilization to the forefront of modern era of technology is being strongly influenced by strong research in the field of artificial intelligence and machine learning. The extensive research works in the field of computing has brought about many changes in human lives by automating many works executed by human beings. The technological progress line has been redefined with the advent of artificial intelligence. The innovative introduction of AI and its allied branches is gradually getting nourished by the spree of research works; as a consequence the new technological concept has been evolving to turn many impossible scenarios into the possible one. AI and its sub-branch of machine learning have enabled the human perception to undergo a sea change, ushering in high precisions in many allied thoughts and ideas and their implementation.

The model based approach for carrying out classification and identification of tasks has led to the pervading progress of machine learning paradigm to diversified fields of technology. Apart from classification process, the machine learning model has become effective enough to predict future trends of any sort of phenomena. Fields like object classification, speech recognition, face detection, etc. have sought extensive application of artificial intelligence and machine learning as well. The application of AI and ML has reached almost all disciplines. The domains of agriculture, health sectors, insurances are some selected few to be influenced directly and indirectly.

Optimization bears a great significance and can be successfully used in shaping up the overall accuracy value to greater height. Operations research is a branch of mathematics that can be used for performing many operational tasks in other allied domains. The implementation of automated strategies in optimization and parameter selection can be carried out by means of artificial intelligence and machine learning. Operations research has many beneficial aspects in decision making. The proper decision depends on a number of factors. The mathematical equation and definition of constraints can easily solve problems and find proper and valid solutions. Automation plays a significant role in minimizing human labor and thereby minimizing overall time and cost. The application of AI and ML has not only provided fruitful solutions, but the concepts can also successfully be used in tackling many problems, typically with good accuracy result.

Artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) are emerging topics in the computer world, and for good reason, as they help companies streamline operations and unearth data so that they can make better business decisions. The business growth is directly or indirectly being managed by proper trend analysis and the predictive approach of growth to the ultimate level of development. The modern economy and its stability are directly being impacted by human labor. The automation of tasks has enabled proper improvement of overall economical structure. Some core concepts and applications like face recognition on smart phones, personalized online shopping experiences, virtual assistants in homes, and even sickness diagnosis have become feasible due to extensive research works in these fields of technology. The demands for these technologies, as well as experts who are familiar with them, are on the rise.

The average number of AI projects in place at a business is likely to more than triple over the next two years, according to a report by research firm Gartner. Organizations face challenges as a result of this exponential expansion. They cite a lack of expertise, producing gross difficulties and worries about the scopes or qualities of business impacted by the technological growth. AI and machine learning, which were once the stuff of science fiction, are now becoming ubiquitous in enterprises. While these technologies are closely linked, there are significant distinctions between them. The novel application of machine learning cum deep learning concept to smooth the automation process for many mathematical models has become the real game changer in the new paradigm of technology.

One of the significant and important problems addressed is computing time reduction which is difficult to overcome. The complexity of any problem in any domain is based on the interrelationships among its involved entities and the effects due to its interactions with others even lying outside the defined peripheries. For example, social distance detection, voice and speech recognition, smooth traffic flow prediction and controlling congestions, and prediction of stock prices. All such emancipation of problems can be mitigated by research contributions gathered into this book which provides enough insights of optimization and its mathematical connections through a research oriented approach towards the novel objective of presenting a conducive societal ambience to human beings. The concepts presented in this book can bring about a unique research direction to the future application of AI-enabled technologies and bring great relief to humanity.

# 1 Predicting Crop Yield Using Quantum Neural Networks

Banu Priya Prathaban

Assistant Professor, Department of Networking and Communications, School of Computing, SRM Institute of Science and Technology, SRM Nagar, Kattankulathur, Chennai, Tamil Nadu, India

DOI: [10.1201/9781032725444-1](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781032725444-1)

## 1.1 Introduction: Background

The agriculture industry and our economy rely heavily on farmers, who are the backbone of our nation. With their wealth of agricultural knowledge passed down through generations, they possess a profound understanding of the most suitable crops for their land \[[1](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_1)\]. Due to abrupt fluctuations in weather, the agricultural industry is unable to predict the approximate output of their produce. By getting to know the average crop yield, farmers can use it help them plan their spending for the current crop season and give them an idea of how much money they may make from selling their produce. Accurate prediction of crop yield before sowing seeds for the current crop season can provide significant benefits to farmers, small organizations, and even the government \[[2](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_2)\].

Artificial intelligence (AI) is the term used to describe the emulation of human intelligence in devices that are programmed to act and think like people. Artificial intelligence (AI) systems are made to learn from experience, adapt to new inputs, and carry out activities that traditionally require human intellect, such as voice recognition, decision-making, and problem-solving \[[3](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_3)\]. AI comes in a variety of forms, such as rule-based systems, machine learning, and deep learning. Rule-based systems make judgments and conduct actions in accordance with a set of predetermined rules. Training a computer program to learn from data and make predictions or judgments using that data is known as machine learning \[[4](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_4)\]. Deep learning is a sort of machine learning that makes use of neural networks to analyze massive volumes of data and discover patterns, enabling the AI system to become more effective over time \[[5](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_5)\]. ML and AI are two related technologies that are being utilized more often in agriculture to enhance crop management and yield prediction \[[6](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_6)\]. AI is the emulation of human intellect in computers that are capable of learning, solving problems, and making decisions–tasks that traditionally require human intelligence. A branch of artificial intelligence called “machine learning” deals with algorithms that automatically learn from experience and get better over time without being explicitly designed \[[7](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_7)\]. With crop yield predictions, farmers can make informed decisions regarding crop selection and planning. They can choose crops that are expected to yield higher and adjust their cultivation practices accordingly \[[8](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_8)\]. This can lead to improved productivity and profitability. Better resource management could be done with the help of such models. Knowing the expected crop yield in advance allows farmers to allocate resources such as seeds, fertilizers, and irrigation more efficiently. They can optimize their inputs based on the predicted yield, reducing wastage and saving costs \[[9](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_9)\].

Uses for AI and ML in agriculture include the following: predicting crop yields based on meteorological information, soil conditions, and other factors, using real-time data to optimize irrigation and fertilizer application, tracking crop growth and health using satellite photography, using picture recognition technology, early pest and disease detection, market trend analysis, crop demand forecasting \[[10](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_10)\]. However, it's important to note that AI and ML are not a panacea for all agricultural challenges. They are just one tool in a farmer's toolkit, and their success depends on the quality of the data and the algorithms used. Ease is achieving risk mitigation \[[11](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_11)\]. Crop yield predictions help farmers mitigate risks associated with uncertain weather conditions. By considering the forecasted yield, farmers can take preventive measures like crop insurance, diversification of crops, or implementing strategies to counter potential yield losses \[[12](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_12)\].

Better market plans can be made as accurate crop yield predictions enable farmers to plan their marketing strategies in advance. They can estimate the supply and demand dynamics, make informed pricing decisions, and negotiate better contracts with buyers. This can help them maximize their profits and minimize losses \[[13](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_13)\]. Government agricultural departments can utilize crop yield predictions to formulate effective policies and programs. They can identify regions or crops that are likely to face yield fluctuations and provide targeted support in the form of subsidies, incentives, or infrastructure development to mitigate the impact \[[14](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_14)\]. Accurate crop yield predictions contribute to overall food security by providing early insights into potential shortfalls or surpluses in crop production. This information can guide the government in managing food reserves, import-export policies, and distribution to ensure a stable food supply for the population \[[15](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_15)\] ([Figure 1.1](#ch01.xhtml#fig1_1)). Use cases of AI in agriculture depicts the use cases of AI in agriculture.

![Figure 1.1 Use cases of AI in agriculture.](images/fig1_1.jpg)

[Figure 1.1 Use cases of AI in agriculture.](#ch01.xhtml#Ffig1_1)

However, accurately predicting crop yield remains a challenge due to unpredictable weather conditions. To address this, a comprehensive methodology has been developed to forecast crop yield, considering parameters such as crop, soil moisture levels, humidity, state, district, crop sowing area, and the specific season of cultivation using Quantum Neural Network (QNN). Also an Agro API is designed in order to obtain the current weather data, including rainfall, temperature, and soil moisture, humidity for accurate predictions. The methodology employed here centers around the prediction of crop yield by considering parameters such as district, state, temperature, area and rainfall. These variables are utilized to develop a prediction model by training a QNN model.

## 1.2 Methodology

Upon thorough examination of prior research and analysis of the current dataset, it has been determined that the effectiveness of features such as crop, rainfall, temperature, soil moisture, humidity, season, and area relies heavily on their segregation by state and district. Therefore, for this type of data, the Quantum Neural Network (QNN) based model is deemed more suitable due to its ability to handle a larger number of categorical values. The QNN algorithm is an excellent choice for crop yield prediction because of its ability to handle both categorical and continuous data, robustness to outliers, and ability to capture nonlinear relationships between the input parameters and the predicted yield. This makes it particularly versatile for predicting crop yield based on a wide range of parameters such as district, rainfall, temperature, state, humidity, season, area, and soil moisture. Additionally, QNNs are easy to understand and interpret, making them ideal for explaining the factors that contribute to the predicted yield. QNN influence the distinctive properties of quantum systems, such as quantum predicament and superposition. The qubits quantum states encrypt the factors and stimulations of the network and quantum gates accomplish quantum processes on the qubits. Their graphical nature makes it easy to visualize how the model arrived at its prediction. Overall, Quantum Neural Network (QNN) is a suitable algorithm for complex prediction tasks in agriculture, where there are often complex and nonlinear relationships between various parameters that can affect crop yield.

Here, two machine learning models have been developed using four different datasets, with two of the datasets being shared between both models. The third dataset in the second model contains extra parameters such as humidity and soil moisture. Similar steps are the followed for both models. To prepare the data for modeling, the three distinct datasets have been merged into a single dataset, which includes both categorical and continuous data. The conversion of categorical data to continuous data is performed via one-hot encoding. This required transforming distinct row values into columns and giving them either a value of 0 or 1. The dataset will then be divided into training and testing halves. A QNN is then used to train the dataset, and the learned model is evaluated using test data. A machine learning system called the QNN is extremely effective in predicting agricultural yields. The QNN is the most effective algorithm for this task for a number of reasons.

Firstly, neural network architecture is easy to understand and interpret, which makes it ideal for explaining the factors that contribute to the predicted yield. QNN models are graphical in nature, making it easy to visualize how the model arrived at its prediction. Secondly, QNN algorithms can handle both categorical and continuous data, making them particularly versatile for predicting crop yield based on a wide range of parameters such as state, district, season, rainfall, temperature, humidity, area, and soil moisture. Thirdly, QNN algorithms can handle nonlinear relationships between the input parameters and the predicted yield, making them particularly suitable for complex prediction tasks. In agriculture, there are often complex and nonlinear relationships between various parameters that can affect crop yield, and QNN can capture these relationships accurately. Finally, QNN algorithms are robust to outliers, which are common in agricultural data due to the wide range of factors that can affect crop yield. This means that QNN models are less susceptible to overfitting and can provide reliable predictions even when the data contains outliers. Overall, the QNN algorithm is an excellent choice for crop yield prediction due to its versatility, interpretability, ability to handle nonlinear relationships, and robustness to outliers.

## 1.3 Quantum Neural Network (QNN)

The Quantum Neural Network (QNN) model incorporates the concepts of quantum computing such as superposition and entanglement into the usual Traditional Neural Network (TNN). Always the smallest unit in a quantum processing system is termed as qubits. Here, the qubits occur in both the 0 and 1 states simultaneously. In QNN, there are qubits known as quantum states that encrypt the factors and stimulations of the network and quantum gates accomplish quantum processes on the qubits. These occurrences help QNN to be used for much more complex problems when compared to the usual TNN. Following are the basic requirements for a QNN model: any binary string can be represented in the form of qubits and basic quantum characteristics such as superposition and entanglement should be considered. Following are the important advantages of QNN over Artificial Neural Network (ANN): parallel processing, exponential memory capability, rapid learning capability, rapid data processing, high stability, reliability and higher accuracy even for lesser hidden neuron count in the model ([Figure 1.2](#ch01.xhtml#fig1_2)). Quantum computing to QNN depicts the evolution of quantum computing into QNN model.

![Figure 1.2 Quantum computing to QNN.](images/fig1_2.jpg)

[Figure 1.2 Quantum computing to QNN.](#ch01.xhtml#Ffig1_2)

QNN has the basic three layers in its model like the traditional NN architecture. It has one input layer, hidden layers and an output layer. Basically in the QNN model, the input neurons, weights and outputs are represented as qubits, as like the estimated quantum states. In our proposed QNN model, there is one input layer, three hidden layers and one output layer. The input data is encoded as input sequences which are arranged using four controlled hadamard gates kept sequentially. In all the five layers, the qubit states are converted over the superposition, rotation and entangled gates. The altered qubit states are estimated by gaining the predictable rate of a Hamiltonian operator of a Pauli gate. The estimated quantities are interpreted with the final outputs. All the parameters of the network are then slowly updated using Adadelt optimizer in our proposed approach ([Figure 1.3](#ch01.xhtml#fig1_3)). Proposed QNN model depicts the model neural network proposed using quantum computing.

![Figure 1.3 Proposed QNN model.](images/fig1_3.jpg)

[Figure 1.3 Proposed QNN model.](#ch01.xhtml#Ffig1_3)

## 1.4 System Design Architecture

System architecture and design refer to the process of designing and planning the overall structure and functionality of a system. It involves defining the components, modules, interfaces, and data that make up the system, as well as the interactions between these elements. The architecture and design of a system are critical to ensuring that it meets the desired performance, scalability, reliability, and maintainability requirements. A well-designed system architecture should be able to handle different scenarios and future changes while meeting the needs of the users and stakeholders. There are four parts in our methodology. The first one is a machine learning model where all the data preprocessing, data merging, training, testing and exporting of the model in pickle format is done. Second part is the backend of a flask where the model is imported, and the input values are formatted properly for a dataset for further prediction. Our final module, the frontend, receives the projected values. Frontend handles all the user activities like taking values from the user, showing predicted values and updated data to the user. Fourth part is NodeJs backend which handles user account details. It handles sign in and login operations by querying the MongoDB database ([Figure 1.4](#ch01.xhtml#fig1_4)). Methodological flow shows the flow of the proposed QNN-based system.

![Figure 1.4 Methodological flow.](images/fig1_4.jpg)

[Figure 1.4 Methodological flow.](#ch01.xhtml#Ffig1_4)

### 1.4.1 ML Model

In this system, two ML models have been created. These ML models are used for predicting crop yield. Four datasets have been used in the two models. The dataset used for each model is an integration of three datasets. This dataset is trained based on the QNN model, which is then exported as a pickle file. The first dataset (crop production.csv has the following parameters - (District\_Name, State\_Name, Crop\_Year, Crop, Season, Area, Production) while crop\_csv\_file dataset has lesser number of states and rows but two more parameters - (Humidity and Soil Moisture). The third dataset (temperatures.csv) has data about temperature values and the fourth dataset has details about rainfall values. In both the models rainfall and temperature dataset have been used along with one of the remaining two datasets. The ML model is trained and tested and exported as a pickle file.

### 1.4.2 Front End

The front end of the application is built using ReactJS, and it displays a form where users can input parameters such as district, state, season, area, and crop. When the “predict” button is clicked, the GPS sends the latitude and longitude to the API, which returns rainfall and temperature details. These parameters are then sent along with other data to the backend for prediction. Users can also navigate through various pages on the homepage, such as Products, Videos, Contact Us, Solutions, Login, and Sign Up. The homepage is designed using HTML and tail wind CSS, and it includes a navigation bar that allows users to move between pages. If a user is not logged in, they will see options to log in or sign up, but if they are logged in, they will see an avatar that displays their email address and a logout option. The Sign Up page contains a form where users can enter personal details, while the Login page allows users to enter their credentials and log in easily.

The Products page displays all available products, and users can add them to their cart or buy them instantly. If a user is logged in, they will see a green coin chip that displays the number of green coins they have earned by giving waste. On the MyCart page, users can view all the products they have added to their cart, along with their prices and a bill card that shows the actual price, discount, and final amount to be paid. They can also redeem coins for discounts on products. The Videos page displays different videos related to organic farming, while the Contact Us page allows users to submit queries related to organic farming or other farming-related issues. Finally, the solutions page allows users to enter details about their district, state, season, area and crop, and the backend utilizes this data to forecast crop yields together with other factors like rainfall, temperature, soil moisture, and humidity. A dialog box appears with information about the predicted crop yield, diseases and their prevention. There is also functionality to check prices for the mandis in the selected crop and district. The max, min, and modal prices of the crop in different mandis based on the user's selected district are displayed.

### 1.4.3 Back End

The back end is made using flask and NodeJs. Flask is used to handle the prediction part of the application whereas NodeJs is used to store the user information and allows it to perform general application functionalities. Flask is used as an API. It consists of two modules. The first module converts the incoming data into a proper tabular format and makes it ready for the prediction and the second module loads the model and predict values from the converted data and sends the results back to the frontend. NodeJs communicates to the MongoDB backend for data storage and retrieval. It authenticates the user while signing up and stores the data in MongoDB database. Similarly, when the user tries to login, it checks whether the input done by the user matches the user info stored in the database.

## 1.5 Model Implementation

QNNs perform better for this sort of data since they contain more categorical values as the bulk of the characteristics which is capable of operating once values are split based on state and district. For the purpose of predicting agricultural yield, two models are built. The second model, which has two additional parameters for humidity and soil moisture, is trained using the crop\_csv\_file. For both datasets, the same model was trained. Data preprocessing refers to the process of cleaning, transforming, and preparing raw data before it is used for analysis. It is an important step in the data analysis pipeline as it can have a significant impact on the quality of the insights gained from the data. The following are some common tasks involved in data preprocessing: Data cleaning: This involves handling missing data, dealing with outliers, and removing duplicated data. Data transformation: This involves scaling, normalization, and encoding of data features. For instance, transforming categorical data into numerical data that can be used for machine learning algorithms. Feature selection: This involves identifying and selecting the most relevant features for a particular analysis, and discarding unnecessary or redundant features. Data integration: This involves merging data from multiple sources to create a single dataset. Data reduction: This involves reducing the dimensionality of the dataset, such as using principal component analysis (PCA) or other dimensionality reduction techniques. Data splitting: This involves splitting the dataset into training, validation, and test sets to evaluate the performance of a machine learning model. PCA is used to reduce the size of a dataset by retaining only the most important principal components. PCA is implemented using Python's scikit-learn library.

To decide the current month from the dataset, rainfall and temperature identification is done. The values in the datasets next, the datasets for temperature and rainfall are aggregated by season, and finally, these datasets are integrated with the datasets for crop production and crop\_csv\_file. One-hot encoding is a method used in data analysis and machine learning to transform categorical data into a format that machine learning algorithms can process more quickly. In order to be employed in machine learning models, categorical variables must be transformed into numerical variables. Each categorical variable is converted into a new binary variable during one-hot encoding, which shows whether or not a specific category is contained in the original variable. Consider a variable called “color” that has the options “red,” “green,” and “blue.” Three new binary variables would be created in a one-hot encoding. If the relevant color is present in the original variable, each variable will take on a value of 1, otherwise it will take on a value of 0. Because many machine learning algorithms can’t handle categorical input directly, one-hot encoding is frequently utilized.

Categorical variables can be transformed into numerical variables so that machine learning algorithms can process and analyze the data more quickly. One-hot encoding ensures that each category is treated equally, which can help to decrease bias in the analysis. One-hot encoding, however, can also produce a significant number of new variables, which can make the data more complex and challenging to analyze. One-hot encoding can also lead to the loss of certain data regarding the connections between categories. One-hot encoding, for instance, would not be able to capture any information about the link. It is significant to highlight that the proposed methodology takes into account the special features of the dataset, especially the abundance of categorical values, which can be difficult to represent with some methods. The dataset can be appropriately prepared for use in a QNN model by carrying out the required data pretreatment processes, including data cleaning, aggregation, and one hot encoding. Following that, forecasts about crop yields depending on various environmental and geographic aspects can be made using the developed model.

The anticipated method is validated using four agricultural datasets such as: one from github \[[16](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_16)\], two from Kaggle \[[17](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_17),[18](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_18)\] and one from the Tamil Nadu government website \[[19](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_19)\]. There are six seasons listed in the season column of the dataset: summer, winter, kharif, autumn, rabi, and annually. Year values have been gathered from 1990 to 2015 for an area of several hectares. Tonnage is used to measure production. The unit of measurement for the monthly and yearly variables are in millimeters (mm). Monthly and yearly temperature data are available in degrees Celsius \[[20](#ch01.xhtml#ref1_20)\] ([Figure 1.5](#ch01.xhtml#fig1_5)). Initial dataset depicts a snap of dataset used in our proposed work.

![Figure 1.5 Initial dataset.](images/fig1_5.jpg)

[Figure 1.5 Initial dataset.](#ch01.xhtml#Ffig1_5)

Final dataset depicts a snap of final dataset used in our proposed work ([Figure 1.6](#ch01.xhtml#fig1_6)). The final dataset is the outcome of preprocessing to get rid of any unnecessary or distracting characteristics and make sure the ones that are left are scaled and normalized correctly. The final dataset ought to contain an adequate number of examples or observations to account for all possible variations in the problem domain. To prevent bias and make sure that the model isn’t overfitting to a certain subset of the data, it should also incorporate a balanced representation of the many groups or categories in the data. To guarantee that the remaining features are properly scaled and normalized, the final dataset should be preprocessed to eliminate any unnecessary or distracting characteristics. The dataset is divided into 70% for training and 30% for testing by means of sklearn in the proposed work implementation. The test images are utilized for testing with the R2 score being used as an accuracy measure. The training dataset is trained on a QNN model with varied parameters. A statistical metric used to assess the effectiveness of a QNN model is the R2 score, also known as the coefficient of determination. It shows the percentage of the dependent variable's volatility that may be accounted for by the independent variable or variables. The model with the highest R2 score value is ultimately chosen. An ideal fit between the model and the data is shown by an R2 score of 1, whereas a score of 0 indicates that the model does not explain any variance in the dependent variable. An R2 score of 0.5 indicates that the independent variable(s) in the model account for 50% of the variance in the dependent variable. R2 = 1 - (SSres / SStot), where SStot is the total sum of squares (the difference between the actual values and the mean value of the dependent variable) and SSres is the sum of squares of residuals (the difference between the actual values and the predicted values). In machine learning applications, the R2 score is a widely used metric to assess the goodness of fit of a QNN model and is frequently used to compare various models or to optimize model parameters ([Figure 1.7](#ch01.xhtml#fig1_7)). Accuracy and loss curves of QNN vs ANN depicts the accuracy and loss curves of QNN. It is very clear that QNN earned an accuracy score of about 0.97, while the traditional ANN gained only 0.88.

## 1.6 Conclusion

A QNN-based agricultural yield prediction system is presented in the proposed work. The model has been designed to focus on a wide range of crops and their expected yield based on various parameters such as location, district, state, temperature, season, and rainfall information. It has been demonstrated that the QNN model employed in this model is capable of accurately forecasting the yield for a mix of definite data. For anticipating the yield, this system has included a new function that uses recent rainfall and temperature data as well as the GPS position of the user at the moment. Additionally, this system may alert users when certain illnesses that could impact particular crops in the dataset are likely to develop. This method has the potential to deliver even more precise crop output projections with the addition of new parameters, making it a useful tool for both farmers and academics.

# 2 A Comprehensive Survey on Risk Factor Monitoring Using Deep Learning Methods on Electrocardiogram Data

Dipanwita Saha

Department of Computer Science and Engineering, Academy of Technology, MAKAUT, Hooghly, West Bengal, India

Gunjan Mukherjee

Department of Computational Sciences, Brainware University, Kolkata, West Bengal, India

Arpitam Chatterjee

Department of Printing Engineering, Jadavpur University, Kolkata, West Bengal, India

Department of Instrumentation & Electronics Engineering, Jadavpur University, Kolkata, West Bengal, India

DOI: [10.1201/9781032725444-2](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781032725444-2)

## 2.1 Introduction

It has been reported by WHO that major deaths are occurring globally due to cardiovascular diseases, while some are even prevented in their early stages by detection with proper diagnosis through the electrocardiogram. The strategies which follow conventional method of ECG checking has become obtrusive and costly, and they even prevent the patient's daily activity.

There are various methodologies for analyzing ECG data through cloud based platforms where wireless transmission techniques are used and the feedbacks are sent to the users. Even here, in any case, where the approaches are to locally prepare the signal of ECG while executing the classifier of arrhythmia in individual smart phones of patients. They allow users to avoid the security issues of cloud and also help in keeping the information of patients in individual gadgets. In the case of persistent observation, the gadgets on the wearable devices are required to do the classification of ECG calculation to be optimized for the objectives of precision and also to minimize the consumption of energy. The main proposal of this current study indicates a neural adaptive method where efficient energy and precise profound neural architecture are demonstrated to identify inconsistencies of ECG arrhythmia for constrained energy stages. Investigation of the wave form of ECG and other morphological characteristics prompts the selection of feature based algorithms which are not sufficient for analyzing different types of ECG signals for patients. The extraction of features are associated with convolutional neural networks (CNN) where these networks are used for classification of different types of ECG beats and fabrication of artery. Moreover, in this paper the energy consumption is viewed as the main point besides the classification of different types of heart beat signals. Previously, there have been various research proposals where IOT based low cost devices are invented for processing of signals in a real time system which has been plotted with cloud system. Previously, it was reported that beat detection can be done by using the wireless ECG data retrieval process, and the same research then were applied with CNN to improve the classifier performance.

## 2.3 Fuzzy Based Approach for ECG Interpretation

Fuzzy is the soft computing techniques used on the basis of membership function. The relevant variables have been utilized with the help of fuzzy membership functions in order to be segregated into a number of distinct classes for the right assessment and interpretation of the ECG graph ([Li et al., 2007](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_15)). The cardiac abnormalities can easily be traced by the fuzzy decision tree techniques ([Behadada & Chikh, 2013](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_3)). The preliminary results for classification purposes through the fuzzy fail to touch the outcome of standard classifiers like the neutral networking ([Behadada & Chikh, 2013](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_3)). An alternative approach towards classification accuracy has been obtained in adaptive fuzzy ECG classifier ([Li et al., 2007](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_15)). The self adaptation procedure has been followed later in order to increase the efficiency of the proposed model implementation carried out by ([Li et al., 2007](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_15)). The multivalve data along with the missing values can be tracked by the fuzzy inference system. Fuzzy expert system has played a crucial role in determining coronary artery diseases. A high level of accuracy has been obtained by applying the fuzzy techniques.

## 2.4 Neural network based approach for interpretation of ECG signal

Neural network is the soft computing approach towards the classification of any object depending on any class. The neural network can successfully be used to classify the ECG signal into different classes after getting through the pre processing stages ([Gao et al., 2010](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_5)) by means of the back propagation techniques with a recorded accuracy value of 90%. A multi channel based ECG signal recording system has been proposed by ([Vishwa et al., 2011](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_24)) for the classification of cardiac arrhythmia with a classification accuracy of 96.78%. A multilayer perceptron network has been proposed by Sao et al. (2015), which incorporated the ANN classifier. A classification with appropriate optimization has been carried out by [Anuradha and Reddy (2008](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_2)) in ANN model. The model was trained on the wavelet based back propagation technique. The achieved accuracy was 90% approximately. [Jadhav et al. (2012](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_10)) designed a model on the statistic back propagation algorithm along with the momentum learning algorithm to follow up the classification issues. [Jadhav et al. (2012](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_10)) proposed a machine learning model using the public dataset for classifying different ECG signals. The introduction of concept of data mining has been found in the work dealing with the analysis of ECG signals by [Gupta and Chatur (2012](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_6)). De Gaetano et al. (2009) have shown the distinction between normal and ischemic beats with a machine learning model based on the radial basis functions and R wave segments.

## 2.5 SVM Based Approaches Towards Classification of ECG Signals

Guler and Ubeyli (2007) proposed a SVM based model for classification of ECG signal received from the brain for mapping of brain image. The ECG signal with the minimum signal to noise ratio has been well explained by SVM classifier in the work of ([Kampouraki et al., 2008](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_13)). The least square support vector machine has been proposed by Polat et al. (2008) for classifying arrhythmiaz. A an accuracy value of 100% has been obtained from the ECG dataset. A variation in the accuracy value has been shown.

## 2.6 Optimization in SVM Based Approaches in classification of ECG Signals

Optimization is a mathematical approach in order to achieve a good result in either the shortest time or with the least number of resources. Since ECG is very much time critical for the diagnosis of ailment type for any patient suffering from a cardiac problem, Wang and Chiang (2007) have shown the application of particle swarm optimization for getting an improved accuracy in case of cardiac diseases. Nasiri et al. (2009) provided a most efficient and feasible solution by means of optimization of feature values. Nasiri et al. (2009) also provided the best performance of SVM classifier with the help of genetic algorithm.

## 2.7 Application of Genetic Algorithms Towards ECG Interpretation

Genetic algorithm is a very effective means in order to achieve a higher computational capability within the least time consuming way (Omran et al., 2009). The genetic algorithm can be utilized in some specific cases for ECG analysis. [Priyadharshini and Kumar (2015](#ch02.xhtml#ref2_19)) carried out one such experiment with the aim of finding the classification of arrythmia from ECG signals. The capability of the GA algorithm over the other corresponding ones has been well exposed in the concerned research paper. The use of other classifiers like the ANN is very prominent in the classification of the ECG signal. Omran et al. (2009) have shown the connecting points of ECG signal and the best possible values were selected through the GA algorithm. The work has shown a drastic reduction in the value over a considerable number of generations. El-Dahshan (2010) applied the GA with wavelet transform for obtaining noiseless signals with the best combination of parameter values. Silveira et al. (2007) have shown the distortion of ECG signals with the help of GA algorithm with good achievement in terms of accuracy value.

## 2.8 Deep Neural Network Approach Towards the Assessment of ECG Signals

The ANN application in assessing ECG signal has been discussed in some of the papers in this regard and has been analyzed in order to get an indepth overview of the ECG signal and its interpretation. Deep learning is an advanced refinement of ANN architecture which consists of multiple numbers of hidden layers. The model is efficient enough to analyze and classify the ECG data represented in the format of images. The supervised learning techniques can be adopted to carry out the classification process. PhysioNet Challenge (2017) data has shown a successful application of the DNN model. The accuracy achieved through such deep learning approach has been estimated at a comparatively higher value.

## 2.9 Application of Machine Learning Algorithm on the ECG Datasets

Machine learning algorithms have been successfully executed on ECG datasets obtained from the open source UCI ML respiratory datasets in this paper. The evaluation of results have been done both on the traditional and machine learning architecture. The models have been executed on the designated datasets in order to detect the myocardial infarction abnormality early on. The deep learning approach for the detection of result has been extended to the classifiers alexnet, resnet 18, alexnet and vggnet 16, respectively. The comparative studies of respective accuracy and loss curves are shown in the following section. The proposed CNN model is shown in [Figure 2.1](#ch02.xhtml#fig2_1).

![Figure 2.1 Proposed CNN model.](images/fig2_1.jpg)

## 2.11 Conclusion

This paper provides an indepth review of the analysis of ECG dataset carried out in a number of different papers. Procedures stated in different papers highlight the detection of several types of cardiac abnormalities from the data obtained from ECG datasets. How the early detection of symptoms on basis of ECG data analysis by machine learning process has led to a fruitful solution, has been stated with elaborate discussion in this paper. The comparative analysis of ECG data analysis with the help of different machine learning algorithms has also been reported in this paper. The relative merit of the ANN model has been shown to be superior to the accuracy scores obtained from other models depending on the concerned ECG datasets. The metrics value obtained has also been in accordance with the result of analysis. Close proximity among accuracy results of different variants of CNN model has also been explicitly expressed and confirms the trends in the case of CNN family in this paper. The currently stated research can be extended to the further analysis through deep learning process in order to obtain much relevant information of different types of cardiac conditions of higher fatality. The accuracy value can also be enhanced by appointing different parameter optimization approaches with suitable fine tuning associated with cardiac images of different stages of heart diseases.

## 3.2 Collection of Data

Because the explanation of acquiring data depends on a questionnaire completed by two writers, let's keep it short and to the point. An addition, the version is in accordance with the instruction. ### 3.2.1 Acquisition of Data

Gathering information, which refers to the process of finding datasets suitable for use in the building of ML models, is the first step to take in the situation of insufficient data. The three techniques for performing this survey are data collection, data enhancement, and information exploration \[[19](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_19)\]. Information identification is the difficulty of categorizing and interpreting datasets. In order to produce fabricated instances, augmenting data manipulates or combines tagged examples. When there is insufficient information accessible, the last option is to create databases on one's own via crowd funding and synthesized production of information methodologies.

#### 3.2.1.1 Data Discovery

Data exploration involves obtaining and categorizing datasets stored either in enterprise information lakes or on the internet. For instance, merchandise is a typical example where algorithms can scan through hundreds of trillions of information sets. Search Goods, for example, analyses information from multiple sources and extracts characteristics into their information lake to maintain a single central information catalog without altering the original datasets. Each entry in the directory includes information detailing the model's behaviors, dimensions, background, production, and consumption \[[16](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_16),[18](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_18)\]. This process encompasses the identification, control, and application of data, including examining data descriptions. According to the scientific advice of Google Schema Search, the data you submit should be accessible on publicly available product versions, ensuring transparency and ease of access.

#### 3.2.1.2 Data Augmentation

Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) are a popular method for generating data in a machine learning environment to enhance information quality. The process starts with a practice set containing real data and involves two key components: a generator, which creates data based on random user input, and a discriminator, which attempts to distinguish between real and generated data. Both the generator and discriminator are trained competitively to improve their performance \[[18](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_18)\]. However, one of the shortcomings of GANs is their occasional inability to produce high-quality data. The generated information can sometimes be significantly different from the real data. To address these inadequacies, various policies and custom modifications by subject matter experts can be implemented. Although these custom modifications may have limitations under certain circumstances, the overall approach remains valid and useful for improving data quality in machine learning applications.

The framework then uses this enhanced data generation to train an initial model version. It examines the information and assesses the precision of the validation set. This outcome determines whether the method provides appropriate information within practical bounds. The body of research on data enhancement is expanding quickly. One aspect of this is addressing uncertainty with straightforward yet effective augmentation methods, such as composing combinations of various data pieces. A cumulative linearity paradigm is employed for anticipating training sets of intermediate data. AI is used to augment the data for specific class subdivisions. The precision of the model is assessed identically for each group, ensuring consistency and reliability in the evaluation process.

#### 3.2.1.3 Data Generation

Another method for gathering or obtaining new knowledge is through generating it. Peer-to-peer fundraising is a popular approach that utilizes online platforms like Amazon Mechanical Turk, where you can either upload the data you need or hire workers to gather it for you. For instance, an employer can direct a worker to complete a specific task. Governmental networks often display portraits of individuals from specific socioeconomic groups, providing valuable data. Additionally, people can use platforms like Thermopolis, a site generator and emulator, to generate and emulate data. This is particularly useful for transport-related movement data aimed at preventing accidents. Diversification by region is effective in enhancing data quality and relevance \[[20](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_20)\]. By modifying the simulator's parameters, you can generate different types of evidence. Researchers have also found that GANs can generate new data, although only a sufficient amount is collected to condition the algorithms effectively.

### 3.2.2 Data Labeling

Once sufficient information is gathered, specimens must be labeled appropriately. This involves discussing record-keeping methods that leverage pre-existing materials, avoiding the need for manual or automatic annotation processes. By utilizing these existing resources efficiently, the labeling process becomes more streamlined and less labor-intensive, ensuring accurate and organized data management.

#### 3.2.2.1 Employing Label Keywords

In conventional tagging, a semi-supervised learning method, the objective is to predict future labels using prior tags. Current machine learning frameworks can be employed to assign sub domains to various activities. The simplest form of this process involves self-learning, where a predictive algorithm is initially built using labeled data and then applied to unlabeled data. The most reliable predictions are accepted as accurate and incorporated into the training process \[[21](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_21)\]. Other approaches, such as shared learning, triangular learning, and knowledge sharing, operate on different principles and do not rely on the same assumptions as this training method. These methods offer alternative strategies for improving the accuracy and efficiency of machine learning models without depending solely on prior labels for future predictions.

#### 3.2.2.2 Labeling Manually without Labels

When there is no clear path to follow but funds can be raised to pay staff, the crowd sourcing technique, such as employing Amazon Mechanical Turk for extra information, is the standard. Though tagging constitutes a crucial chore, labeling alternatives have arisen, including those from Google's Labeling, an Amazon-owned firm, and Grange manufacturer Grounded Truth \[[17](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_17)\]. Users can select the recording operation and find the logbook that collects information with the help of Sage maker's user-friendly interface and technological capabilities. When participants lack the essential abilities to succeed, crowd fundraising may occasionally not be practical.

Therefore, hiring professional recording is the final option, but it could be expensive. The goal is to encourage people to save unlabeled samples, which will improve the model's capacity to forecast. Active learning is a great way to lower the cost of information collection. Even when actively learning is not a possible, the choice of model may frequently be reduced to an assessment of dubious sampling and the use of conceptual assumptions to confirm the influence of the new set of data on the correct framework.

#### 3.2.2.3 Labelless Auto-Tagging

Creating unmarked labels (thus the phrase “unlabeled” tags) (semi)automatically is a feature of tagless auto-tagging, which is currently gaining popularity.

### 3.2.3 Increasing Existing Knowledge

In addition to documenting, checking credentials can improve current standards and knowledge. This method is required in many circumstances. No matter how new or important the program's objectives, the lack of external data or the program's poor quality preclude it from being useful for enhancing the performance of the predictive model. In this case, it would be preferable to increase the knowledge that is currently available. Redundancy is a fantastic way to improve labeling.

There are both situations and infrastructures where the model's accuracy holds steady over time, independent of new data while the data's quality declines \[[22](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_22)\]. Given this circumstance, improving label excellence—which can be done by repeatedly consistently commenting on a number of labels for a specific specimen—is the simplest technique to raise the model's precision. In actuality, anyone can delete it all, especially the notes, which naturally leads to the discussion of information accessibility, maintenance, and compatibility.

## 3.3 Preservation, Integration, and Validation of Data

There are numerous errors in this training-related information. By using visual analysis and modeling approaches, validation of information characteristics in artificial intelligence frameworks like Topology Extensions can quickly discover information flaws. Through information cleaning, actual data can be handled, and there are numerous information kinds with equivalent integrity. However, a recent study has shown that prior to training algorithms, cleaning data by error correction is not necessarily advantageous for artificial intelligence.

Contrarily, sterilizing the machine used for learning is more advantageous than retraining an algorithm because it will boost reliability and make the system more resistant to input disturbance \[[14](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_14)\]. A new study suggests that building strength through training is more effective than cleaning data before building models. In the field of protection, auditory information that has been cleaned is frequently referred to as clean information. If speech data contains toxic compounds, it could potentially be dangerous. Because biased data may produce discriminatory algorithms for training, the incorporation of artificial intelligence principles, including modeling integrity, offers an extra problem. The scientific review hasn’t yet gone into great detail on toughness or impartiality retraining, but computational learning environment has done substantial research in these areas.

### 3.3.1 Validating the Data

Visualized data is a popular and effective method of information verification for machine learning applications. Visualization, as opposed to traditional information cleansing, enables users to quickly yet completely check data to stop more major errors from happening later. Routes is an illustration of a freeware program that shows a variety of data points and statistical data that may be used for tracking the data to stop more significant problems. Research is being done on the creation of new visualizations that can be used practically in addition to hand visualization.

#### 3.3.1.1 Validation Based on Schemas

Tensor flow is in order to exploit prospective information as well as warn consumers of incorrect information; validation of information builds information architecture from previous information. With every exception, TFDV presents specific goods to address the root problem. Compared to the typical relational structure, which describes statistical information attributes, this template is different \[[12](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_12),[13](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_13),[14](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_14),[15](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_15)\]. The end-user is left in charge of deciding whether the newly added information breaches the present regulation, whether it needs to be fixed, or whether the structure of the database needs to be amended. This information evaluation technology was recently provided with further capabilities. Artificial intelligence process assessment for published material is made possible by the Mlinspect library. Other benefits comprise issue type classification that is autonomous, assessing the impact of model mistakes, convenience of usage, and rapid person evaluation.

### 3.3.2 Information Cleansing

According to the researchers, enhancing the core educational needs through information purging is useless. In actuality, cleaning occasionally affects the structure. It is possible to lessen the negative impacts of information filtering by choosing the appropriate neural network model. Additionally, because there isn’t just one best cleaning method, the strategy should be determined by the type of noise that needs to be eliminated. Numerous essential maintenance details, such as boundaries that must be changed—a process similar to machine learning's parameter tuning—can potentially have undesirable effects \[[20](#ch03.xhtml#ref3_20)\]. Therefore, it is appropriate to exercise caution when applying information cleansing techniques not designed for use by artificial intelligence. Recently, information-erasing algorithms have been created.

Soiled documents are recreated using a program called Active clean, which also updates the template. A sample is selected by the simulation to be affected, and the infected sample is subsequently identified using high-quality information, as shown in [Figure 3.3](#ch03.xhtml#fig3_3). The information is chosen even though it would be extremely expensive to maintain all of it. Any model may be vacuumed by a qualified specialist or an observer. When the next version is selected, the representation is then improved for accuracy. Theoretically, Active clean guarantees that the machine learning algorithm will only be as accurate as if it were first trained on fresh data by copying an impeccable yet previously optimized system.
