## 21.1 Introduction

Crop diseases represent a substantial threat to the stability of our food supply, with producers bearing the lion's share of the load. Agriculture is essential to the natural, social, and biological futures of the global population. Agricultural diseases are the leading cause of most leaf damage, lowering farming standards. To overcome the burden of the farmers, manual monitoring is replaced with automated monitoring. Timely detection of onset of diseases and rapid detection are required to prevent severe damage to crops. Smart farming concept makes use of a variety of sensors in the agricultural field to collect data for monitoring the growth of plants. With the increased use of high-end cameras and other technological devices, image processing-based plant disease diagnoses are increasing in popularity. Effective segmentation and feature extraction methods are also required for automatic plant disease detection. It is possible to improve the accuracy by segmenting the accurate region of interest, i.e. the diseased area, and extracting characteristics relating to the infected area.

One of the main issues in agriculture is the loss of natural resources, which will prevent food production from keeping up with the growing worldwide population. Regardless of the difficult climate factors, enhancing efficiency is the main concern in this situation. Utilising the most recent agricultural technological developments, smart farming increases output. Image capture, segmentation, feature extraction, and ML algorithms are employed in the automated plant leaf diagnostic process for crop surveillance. By delivering a rapid and exact diagnosis of the plant state, an automatic disease identification approach speeds up the diagnostic procedure. To speed up crop diagnosis, the illness detection process needs to be optimised. Image processing is the process of making images better and getting rid of noise. Data preprocessing is currently developing swiftly. Techniques for image processing include things like augmentation, segmentation, feature extraction, categorisation, and other things.

Adjusting the brightness, colour temperature, and contrast are all steps in the image-refining process. The technique of segmenting an image involves dividing it into manageable pieces. The procedure is commonly used to find things in digital images. Vision methods for segmentation include transform-based, texture-based, thresholding, and colour-based methods. The extracting characteristics technique minimizes the number of pixels in the end product by deleting just the most relevant and evident components of a picture. By employing a big picture size and a low feature representation, this strategy speeds up image matching and retrieval. The classification of images is the process of categorising images into one of many specified categories.

Agriculture data preprocessing, a significant application of image analysis, is the research branch in the discipline with the highest growth. The processing of images has been shown to be effective for analyzing information in a range of sectors, particularly farming. Cameras, aircraft, or satellites capture images, which are subsequently processed. Machines modify and analyze these images using image processing techniques. Due to recent advancements in image capture and data processing technologies, resolving a variety of difficulties in agriculture has never been simpler. In smart agriculture, images would be used to quantify the affected area, describe the colour, form, and severity of the disease, as well as to identify infected leaves, stems, and fruits.

This chapter presents a technique that uses artificial intelligence and image processing to find leaf disease on plants. The system can take a leaf-like image as input. Environmental noise elimination is the first step in preparing images of leaves. Use the mean filter to reduce ambient noise. Histogram equalization enhances the image's quality. Segmentation in image is the splitting of an image into numerous portions or segments. It aids in defining the limits of the image. Following the process of segmentation classification is carried out utilising methods such as SVM, Random forest classification, and K Nearest Neighbour.

## 21.2 Literature Survey

A hybrid forecasting model was created in this work ([Lamba et al., 2023](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_12)) utilising images of wounded crops to predict varied levels of blast disease severity. The one that was recommended is a severity prediction model with four levels. The degree of severity is determined by the proportion of the leaf's outermost layer affected by the illness. The illustration dataset was created using sources that were both secondary and primary. To retrieve the characteristics at the start, Convolutional Neural Network technology is employed. The degree of seriousness of the blast illness is then identified and classified using a Support Vector Machine. There are a total of 1908 pictures in the collection. The suggested hybrid model has an accuracy of 97%.

The investigators ([Shantkumari et al., 2023](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_18)) use machine learning algorithms to distinguish between diverse disease categories and diagnose grape leaf disease early. For the classification of leaf diseases, an improved K-Nearest Neighbour model and a neural network-based Classification model are also presented. The high-quality gradient-based features that arise are subsequently employed in the classification step. The suggested approaches significantly enhance classification precision. The recommended models are tested using the Plant-Village Dataset, a publicly accessible dataset. The recommended models outperform several popular classification methods in terms of classification accuracy.

Discovering nutrients that are deficient or plentiful in the leaf is a crucial issue in agriculture for enhancing crop production and productivity. Several image processing algorithms for recognising and minimizing insufficiency in plant leaves have been developed by various academics. ([Janani and Jebakumar, 2023](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_8)). Some of these methods of identification have difficulty being adaptable, highly costly, that can lead to crop irreversibility if the nutrient content of a leaf is not discovered early on. As the prediction process grows more advanced, erroneous predictions degrade crop quality and yield. The Analysis of Nutrient Ranges For identifying and categorising nitrogen nutrients in groundnut leaves, the Greenness technique is recommended. The proportion of their surroundings in peanut leaves was calculated using colour features and shown to be highly related to nitrogen nutrition. Following that, the suggested Convolution Neural Network-based Recurrent Vector Network method recognises and divides the three nutritional ranges. According to the results, the CNN-based HVN system built achieved 95% precision during training as well as 92% validating efficiency.

In this paper ([Narla and Suresh, 2023](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_14)), a method is created that employs digital image processing to detect plant sickness from the leaf, since disease of plants appears on the leaf's surface. The three pipelining procedures employed in the DIP approach are preprocessing, segmentation, and feature extraction. The sick leaf is used to create texture, colour, and shape characteristics. Following that, the learned attributes are utilised to train an SVM classifier to identify distinct ailments. Tests on tomato plant images produced from traditional data sets revealed an accuracy of 86.6% when cross-validated.

Researchers provide Former Leaf, a transformer-based leaf disease detection model, as well as two optimisation methodologies to increase model performance in this paper ([Thai et al., 2023](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_19)). Using this method, the model size can be reduced by 28%, accuracy can be increased by 3% and evaluation time can be increased by 15%. In order to measure matrix correlation, sparse matrices and matrix multiplication are applied to the model. By reducing the model complexity, the training time can be reduced by 10% while the performance remains the same. Cassava Leaf Infection Data The evaluation results show that our technique outperforms the latest mathematical models in many situations.

The ultimate goal of the research (see [Liu et al., 2022](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_13)) is to use the IOT to directly detect environmental factors in fields and create machine learning technology for early disease detection. The life cycles of plant diseases are closely related to environmental factors. Plant disease incidence is predicted using field environmental parameters. Using environmental factors from the connection to the internet of agricultural fields, machine learning algorithms can forecast the onset of plant diseases with high accuracy.

The authors ([Jumat et al., 2018](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_10)) recommend developing a model for low-cost smart farming that can detect plant illnesses early and warn farmers so that preventative actions may be adopted. Additionally, a system that can be accessed online has been created to let farmers keep tabs on the health of the plants in their greenhouses. Farmers will be able to spot illnesses in their plants at an early stage due to the development agricultural leaf disease detection system, reducing production loss due to a full-blown disease outbreak in their greenhouse. One of the system's essential advantages is its ability to categorise the Septoria plant disease, for which defining the stages of disease breakout on the leaf is critical. Furthermore, researchers demonstrated that the Raspberry Pi could conduct categorisation using a machine-learning technique without compromising speed. Our study also shows that a random forest classifier combined with a colour histogram is the ideal configuration for this prototype to employ for successful plant disease diagnosis.

Farmers may use autonomous disease identification devices to recognise plant illnesses fast and accurately. To speed up crop diagnostics, methods for detecting foliar diseases in plants must be automated ([Zamani et al., 2022](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_21)). Research presented a method for identifying leaf disease using computer methods such as picture analysis. This framework may accept a leaf picture as input. First, noise in leaf images is removed during preprocessing. To lower noise levels, use conventional filtering. Histogram equalization improves image quality. The split of a single point of view into several parts or portions is known as segmentation in photography. It helps define image borders. The image is segmented utilising the K-Means technique. The machine learning technique uses significant element analysis to extract features.

The authors ([Raghuvanshi et al., 2022](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_16)) developed a mechanism for identifying and characterizing intrusions targeting agricultural IoT networks. For this physical prototype in the proposed technique, data is initially preprocessed by being converted into numerical attributes. Cluster analysis is used to extract characteristics. Machine learning techniques are used to identify precompiled retrieved data.

In this investigation ([Harakannanavar et al., 2022](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_7)), diseased tomato plant specimens are taken into account. Farmers can quickly diagnose infections based on early signs using tomato leaf disorder samples. To improve the standard of the tomato samples, the leaves of tomatoes were initially shrunk to 256 \* 256 pixels and then histogrammed equalized. Several classifiers are used to extract significant features from leaf extracts. The incoming data was processed using various machine learning methods.

Compressed sensing (CS) is a method that the author ([Aasha Nandhini et al., 2018](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_1)) recommends for employing to detect and diagnose diseases in crops. The strategy was examined utilising simulation and experimental analysis for a number of plants. The target area of the leaf image is separated from the surrounding environment by an improved K-means segmentation algorithm. Once the characteristics are extracted based on the aspects, by using ML algorithm. [Archana et al. (2022](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_2)) present a unique intensity based colour picture retrieval method for obtaining the textural qualities. In contrast, the dimension and width of the affected parts establish structural qualities. The photos are subsequently classified in order to find different attribute values using the revolutionary support vector computing Bayesian neural network approach.

Deep learning has helped recent advancements in artificial intelligence open the way for the recognition of plant leaves by capturing them and utilising them as the basis for differentiating between various plant species. This research ([Bandyopadhyay et al., 2022](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_3)) introduced a novel way for classifying distinct plant categories. Deep learning models were trained on images from the Plant Village dataset to detect foliar diseases.

Computer vision was utilised ([Jhuria et al., 2013](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_9)) to grade fruit and identify diseases. They take into account, among other things, morphology, colour, and texture. The morphological traits stand out among the rest. Grapes can develop apple scab, rot, and black rot in addition to powdery mildew and black rot.

There are numerous approaches to recognise and group plant and fruit diseases. A number of watermelon leaf diseases, including anthracnose and Downey mildew, were categorised by ([SuhailiKutty et al., 2013](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_11)). The RGB colour components of a sample of an infected leaf must be used to pinpoint the region of interest in order to accomplish this. Mean filters were employed by the authors to eliminate noise from the input data. ([Dubey and Jalal et al., 2012](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_6)) analysed three of several apple disorders.

The study's findings revealed a high level of efficiency. Support vector machine and pattern classification were employed by researchers to distinguish first cercospora leaf patches in sugar beet plants (SVM). To ensure accuracy, they followed a three-step process. Continuous measurement in daylight enables on-site detection and classification of plant disease.

([Sannakki et al., 2011](#ch21.xhtml#ref21_17)) made an effort to categorise and identify disorders using neural networks. The author described a testing method for determining the condition while masking it as an intelligent system. The study on grapes suggests dividing this system into two independent phases. Initially, the image is used to identify the objec; using the segmentation method mentioned, this object detection is carried out. In the second step, image masking and disease prediction may be combined. The authors used the K-means clustering technique to identify and categorise diseases.

## 21.3 Proposed Ensemble Learning Framework

In this part, we'll talk about a straightforward and unique automated plant leaf disease detection framework. The system contains an integrated camera sensor that will take pictures and process them for classification. As the HSI model detects colour similarly to how a human does, the photos are preprocessed utilising colour transformation models like RGB to HSI.

Following colour transformation, the Hue component is employed to separate the portion of the leaf afflicted by the disease from the healthy leaf. The sick regions are segmented, and features from the Histogram of Gradients (HoG) are retrieved for classification. For classification, many ML models such as SVM, RFC, and KNN are used. The proposed segment and feature technique for classifying plant diseases uses the Stacked Ensemble Learning-Based Model, which integrates three machine learning algorithms, SVM, KNN, and RFC, as well as modified logistic regression in the Meta layer. Finally, stacked ensemble learning is compared to existing state-of-the-art ML approaches. [Figure 21.1](#ch21.xhtml#fig21_1) displays the architecture of the suggested paradigm.

![Figure 21.1 Proposed ensemble model.](images/fig21_1.jpg)

[Figure 21.1 Proposed ensemble model.](#ch21.xhtml#Ffig21_1)

The HOG feature vector is transmitted to the cloud, and the tracking head uses the stacked ensemble learning method for classification. According to the study, the use of a stacked general model consisting of machine learning in the underlying layer and logistic regression can improve the accuracy of disease prediction. In first stage, the predictions of each machine learning model are merged given as input to the secondary classifier which makes used to predict the output. The predicted output is used to train the secondary classifier (meta layer) which is a logistic regression. The final output of stacked ensemble learning is the classified disease. To validate the proposed system pomegranate leaves are used and performance metrics such as Recall, Precision, F1 score and accuracy are used to analyze the efficiency of the system.

The steps involved in the classification of diseased leaves using the proposed NSP framework are given below:

1.  Step 1: Image preparation, which includes removing complex backgrounds, shrinking images, and grayscale conversion.
2.  Step 2: Segment the disease affected leaf region using Proposed Pixel Replacement Based Segmentation.
3.  Step 3: Extracting HOG characteristics from the ROI.
4.  Step 4: Classification of leaf diseases with efficient classifiers such as RFC, KNN, and SVM.
5.  Step 5: Analyze the effectiveness of stacked ensemble classifiers for the proposed method.

The proposed work's several processes are described in full further below.

### 21.3.1 Crop Image Acquisition

Images of plant leaves were obtained from the Plant Village Dataset for processing, segmentation, and classification. These images are in RGB (red, green, and blue) format. For ease of calculation, every image utilised in the analysis is preprocessed by transforming it to greyscale image. Input image is denoted as S(x,y) where x and y are the coordinates of the pixel. Equation (21.1) is utilised in the proposed work for colour to grey scale conversion:

*G**r**e**y* *I**m**a**g**e* *S*[*R**G**B*](*x*,*y*) = *S*[*R*](*x*,*y*) + *S*[*G*](*x*,*y*) + *S*[*B*](*x*,*y*) (21.1)

where S[R](x,y), S[G](x,y), and S[B](x,y) are the intensities of a colour image in red, green, and blue. The hues in a grayscale picture are shades of grey. The change is due to the fact that each pixel now requires less data. The RGB contains three separate values, with a value range of 0–255.

### 21.3.2 Crop Image Preprocessing

The input image is initially resized before colour transforming the image. The input image is in RGB format and it will be converted to HSV. The HSV colour model, which derives its name from the acronym (Hue, Saturation, Value), is a non-linear modification of the RGB colour model in which the colours are a mixture of three values: Hue, Saturation or quantity of colour, and their value.

To convert an RGB image to an HSV image following conversion, Equations (21.2), (21.3) and (21.4) are used

*S*[*R**G**B*](*x*,*y*)→*S*[*H**S**V*](*x*,*y*)

*H* *C**o**m**p**o**n**e**n**t*→*S*[*H*](*x*,*y*) (21.2)

*S* *C**o**m**p**o**n**e**n**t*→*S*[*S*](*x*,*y*) (21.3)

*V* *C**o**m**p**o**n**e**n**t*→*S*[*V*](*x*,*y*) (21.4)

### 21.3.3 Novel Segmentation Process (NSP)

The technique computes a statistical threshold and compares each pixel against it in order to find the pixels that belong to the affected region. In this research, the disease-affected portion of the picture is separated from the rest of the image using a statistical threshold-based technique known as Novel segmentation process (NSP). The preprocessed picture goes through a block division procedure where each image is split into n x n blocks after the input image M x N is downsized.

### 21.3.4 Classification

Finally, after extracting the required features from all of the images, they are fed to the classifier. Features from train dataset are used to train the model and the test class to evaluate the model. Three learning algorithms are used in this work: Random Forest Classifier (RFC), K-Nearest Neighbours (KNN) and Support Vector Machine (SVM).

#### 21.3.4.1 Support Vector Machine

SVM (Support Vector Machine) is a supervised machine learning technique. SVM can be applied to both binary and multivariate classification. Multivariate SVM is used in this work because there are more than two classes to predict. SVM can handle many categorical and continuous data elements. The goal of the SVM method of classification is to find the largest marginal hyper plane in the N-dimensional space that clearly classifies the data points. Support vectors are iteratively built at the endpoints of the compared data points to minimize mistakes. The marginal distance is the distance between two parallel support vectors. Support vectors are arranged in such a way that the marginal distance is minimized and the vectors are parallel to one another. Then, a hyper plane is constructed between the support vectors to classify the data points. SVM is a popular classification technique because it achieves high accuracy while using little computational effort.

#### 21.3.4.2 K-Nearest Neighbours (KNN)

The K-Nearest Neighbour (KNN) algorithm is a simple supervised machine learning approach. KNN may be used for both classification and regression. KNN is used for multivariate classification in this study. KNN assumes similarity between new incoming data points and existing data points and then assigns new data points to appropriate classes based on the similarity of previously available data points. The most crucial stage in KNN is determining the value of K. A small number of K can result in data point overfitting, whereas a large value of K can be computationally costly.

#### 21.3.4.3 Random Forest Classifier

Random forest is a supervised learning approach based on decision trees. Random forest, unlike decision trees, may be used for both classification and regression. Entropy and the Gini Index are used in decision trees to choose the appropriate characteristic to split the tree. The leaf node in the tree is made up of labelled data. Overfitting is frequently caused by the decision tree. Random forests can handle both numerical and categorical data, unlike decision trees, and do not experience overfitting of their training data set. In Random Forest, N characteristics are chosen at random from among the M available features/attributes.

#### 21.3.4.4 Ensemble Learning Classification

By mixing many methods, ensemble learning improves a learning model's ability to anticipate outcomes. Global research efforts are focused on developing effective ensemble ML algorithms for modelling, prediction, and analytics issues. The studies show that an ensemble performs more effectively than a single learner in terms of classification and prediction accuracy. As it uses several classifiers and tries to improve the performance of base classifiers separately, ensemble learning is a strong technique in machine learning. Despite the fact that it can't always ensure success, it typically lowers variance and provides higher performance than a single classifier solution. An ensemble classifier assists in examining the danger of receiving subpar results from a single classifier system by selecting a certain aggregation strategy, such as majority voting, boosting, bagging, etc.

#### 21.3.4.5 Stacked Ensemble Learning

The typical structure of a stacked ensemble consists of two or more base models and a higher-level meta model, with the following functions for each model. Models that predict data outside of the sample and fit the training set are called base models. A model that adjusts to base-model predictions and finds the best approach to include the predictions is referred to as a meta-model. In this method, many models, often of various sorts, are combined. The output of the Level (N-1) classifiers will be utilised as training data for a Level N classifier to approximate the exact same target function.

Primary classifiers and secondary classifiers make up the majority of the algorithm. There is no restriction on the number or variety of primary classifiers. However, the straightforward classical and heterogeneous classifiers are favoured in terms of effectiveness and generalizability. In order to build the stacking ensemble model in this study, two classifiers, such as SVM as stated in [Chapter-3](#ch03.xhtml#chapter3) and MF-EDL as discussed in [Chapter-4](#ch04.xhtml#chapter4), were utilised as the major classifiers and logistic regression as the secondary classifier. All primary classifiers were trained during the initial training phase to produce high-accuracy classifiers and their outputs. The outputs were then combined as the training data for the secondary logistic regression in the second training step.

#### 21.3.4.6 Logistic Regression

The classification problem is what logistic regression addresses rather than the regression problem. It is a conventional linear classifier that trains on positive and negative examples in the data to learn sample features in order to produce hypothetical functions between labels. A cost function is created, an optimisation approach is employed to find the most effective variables, and the model is eventually tested against the categorisation problem. In this experiment, the loss function was penalized using L2 regularization. There was a penalty term added to the parameters after the loss function. This prevented the model from becoming overfitted by keeping it simple and limiting the number of parameters.

## 21.4 Results and Discussion

Python was used to implement every framework in CoLab's GPU runtime. Images of apple plant leaves were used to extract and classify symptoms including apple scab, apple black rot, apple cedar rust, and healthy apple. Both training and test pictures were present in the database. SVM is the classification algorithm used.

Using a colour modelling approach, the input image, which is 256 ×256 in size, was converted from RGB to HSV. Segmentation threshold is established by computing the mean and variance for each of the components. The disease-affected area is segmented using the thresholds before the HOG features are retrieved. [Figures 21.2](#ch21.xhtml#fig21_2), [21.3](#ch21.xhtml#fig21_3) and [21.4](#ch21.xhtml#fig21_4) show disease affected apple leaves, HSV transformed images of apple diseases and sick leaf segmented using the proposed technique. [Figure 21.5](#ch21.xhtml#fig21_5) shows a comparison of the proposed ensemble system and machine learning for apple leaf disease.

In order to verify the effectiveness of the suggested structure, both detection and classification accuracy are put to the test and the results are compared to earlier attempts. Recall and precision are utilised to calculate detection accuracy using Equations (21.5), (21.6), and (21.7).

*R**e**c**a**l**l* = *T**r**u**e* *p**o**s**i**t**i**v**e* *v**a**l**u**e*/*T**r**u**e* *p**o**s**i**t**i**v**e* *v**a**l**u**e* + *F**a**l**s**e* *N**e**g**a**t**i**v**e* *v**a**l**u**e* (21.5)

*P**r**e**c**i**s**i**o**n* = *T**r**u**e* *P**o**s**i**t**i**v**e* *v**a**l**u**e*/*T**r**u**e* *p**o**s**i**t**i**v**e* *v**a**l**u**e* + *F**a**l**s**e* *p**o**s**i**t**i**v**e* *v**a**l**u**e* (21.6)

*A**c**c* = 2 \* (*R**e**c**a**l**l*\**p**r**e**c**i**s**i**o**n*)/(*R**e**c**a**l**l*+*p**r**e**c**i**s**i**o**n*) (21.7)

## 21.5 Conclusion

A Stacked Ensemble learning based automated plant disease detection system is developed in this paper to efficiently identify plant diseases. To realize this RGB to HSI colour transformation model, segmentation and Histogram of Gradient features are used. Three learning algorithms are used in this work i.e. RFC, KNN and SVM. For implementation to validate the proposed methodology the apple plant is chosen and images from various categories were taken from the plant village dataset. The approach was applied in Python, and subsequently, metrics like recall, precision, and F1 score were used to evaluate how effectively the proposed methodology performed. In comparison to other effective stacked ensemble models the results show that the Histogram of Gradients features and stacked delivered 98.7% accuracy. The outcomes are encouraging, and going forward, a hardware prototype will be used to validate the framework. The prototype will also be used in the real world for testing and validation.
